```json
    "newString": "joint spectral-downstream tracking~\\cite{gu2026sigma} is essential to detect early collapse.\nThe value of SLV lies not in temporal precedence over downstream metrics, but in requiring no text generation or external benchmark evaluation, enabling continuous monitoring at training time.\n\n\\paragraph{Phase II:",
    "originalFile": "\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{subsec:setup}\n\nWe use Pythia-410M-deduped \\cite{biderman2023pythia} as our base model and fine-tune on a 50K-subset of OpenWebText.\nStarting from the pretrained checkpoint (generation~0), each subsequent generation fine-tunes on synthetic text produced by the previous generation, for a total of 10 recursive generations.\nAt each generation, we compute spectral metrics on the embedding Gram matrix $\\mathbf{G} = \\mathbf{E}^\\top \\mathbf{E}$ from 1K held-out samples, where $\\mathbf{E} \\in \\mathbb{R}^{n \\times d}$ stacks last-layer embeddings.\nWe track three spectral indicators: \\textit{effective rank} $r_{\\text{eff}} = \\exp(H(\\mathbf{p}))$, where $H(\\mathbf{p})$ is the Shannon entropy of the normalized singular value distribution; \\textit{spectral log-volume} (SLV) $\\sum_i \\log \\sigma_i = \\frac{1}{2}\\log \\det \\mathbf{G}$; and the leading singular value $\\sigma_1$.\nWe pair these with three downstream metrics: perplexity, HellaSwag accuracy (0-shot), and distinct-4.\n\nFor the mixing comparison (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase1}), we evaluate four strategies sharing the same total real-data budget:\n(1)~\\textbf{pure collapse} ($r_t{=}0$),\n(2)~\\textbf{fixed mix} ($r_t{=}0.50$),\n(3)~\\textbf{linear increasing} ($r_t$: $0.20 \\to 0.80$), and\n(4)~\\textbf{adaptive} ($k{=}10$, Eq.~\\ref{eq:controller}).\n\n\\subsection{Spectral Collapse Characterization}\n\\label{subsec:phase0}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:phase0} reports spectral and downstream metrics across 10 recursive generations.\nThe trajectory reveals a three-phase structure in the spectral dynamics of model collapse.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}rrrrrrc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGen & $r_{\\text{eff}}$ & SLV & PPL & HeSw & D-4 & $\\sigma_1$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n0  & 417.55 & 2366.68 &  9.55 & .413 & .741 & 1942.8 \\\\\n2  & \\textbf{496.44} & \\textbf{2469.93} & 13.58 & .376 & .515 &  712.0 \\\\\n5  & 479.21 & 2370.17 & 24.59 & .344 & .463 &  741.0 \\\\\n7  & 464.20 & 2304.19 & 38.89 & .319 & .447 &  791.3 \\\\\n10 & 442.46 & 2178.95 & 76.78 & .295 & .426 &  825.7 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Spectral and downstream metrics at selected generations (Pythia-410M; full trajectory described in text). Bold marks the spectral peak before collapse onset.}\n\\label{tab:phase0}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Phase I: Spectral Decompression (Gen 0--2).}\nWe identify phase boundaries at inflection points where $r_{\\text{eff}}$ and SLV transition from rising to declining.\nIn the first two generations, $r_{\\text{eff}}$ rises from 417.55 to 496.44 (+18.9\\%) and SLV increases from 2366.68 to 2469.93 (+4.4\\%), while $\\sigma_1$ drops sharply from 1942.8 to 712.0, reflecting a redistribution of spectral mass from the dominant direction to smaller components.\nCrucially, this decompression does not indicate improved model quality: HellaSwag drops from 0.413 to 0.376 ($-$8.9\\%) and distinct-4 falls from 0.741 to 0.515 ($-$30.5\\%) during the same interval.\nHellaSwag already declines 5.6\\% from Gen~0 to Gen~1, precisely when spectral indicators are still rising, implying that decompression captures distributional broadening rather than preservation of task-supporting representations.\nA monitoring system relying solely on spectral metrics during Phase~I would incorrectly conclude the model remains healthy; joint spectral-downstream tracking~\\cite{gu2026sigma} is essential to detect early collapse.\n\n\\paragraph{Phase II: Collapse Onset (Gen 2--4).}\nBoth $r_{\\text{eff}}$ and SLV reverse after Gen~2 ($r_{\\text{eff}}$: 496.44$\\to$493.31, SLV: 2470$\\to$2441), marking the transition from decompression to collapse while perplexity continues its monotonic rise.\n\n\\paragraph{Phase III: Accelerating Collapse (Gen 4--10).}\nSpectral contraction accelerates: the mean per-generation percentage $r_{\\text{eff}}$ decline in Phase~III is 2.43$\\times$ the onset-generation rate (Gen~2$\\to$3).\nBy Gen~10, $r_{\\text{eff}}$ falls to 442.46 ($-$10.9\\% from peak), SLV drops to 2178.95 ($-$7.9\\% from baseline), perplexity reaches 76.78 ($+$704\\%), and $\\sigma_1$ reverses its earlier decline (700.7$\\to$825.7), indicating renewed spectral concentration (Figure~\\ref{fig:spectral_trajectory}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Scale Validation}\n\\label{subsec:cross_scale}\n\nRepeating the protocol on Pythia-1B (Gen~0--3) confirms the same decompression-onset structure ($r_{\\text{eff}}$ peak at Gen~2, SLV peak one generation earlier), with SLV's low cost and monotonic post-peak decline making it well-suited as an automated collapse monitor.\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Mixing Comparison}\n\\label{subsec:phase1}\n\nUnder pure synthetic training (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}), SLV peaks at Gen~2 (2470) and declines monotonically thereafter.\nWith 50\\% real-data mixing, SLV rises over six of seven generations, reaching 2526 at Gen~7 (57 points above the pure-collapse peak); a negligible dip at Gen~1 ($\\Delta_1 \\approx 0.0003$, well below $\\tau$) reflects the initial fine-tuning transient.\n\nThe adaptive controller ($k{=}10$) remains silent through all seven generations: $\\Delta_t$ is non-positive from Gen~2 onward, and the sole positive value ($\\Delta_1 \\approx 0.0003$) falls $6\\times$ below $\\tau{=}0.002$, leaving the $\\max(0, \\Delta_t - \\tau)$ gate closed.\nFrom Gen~2 onward, $\\tau$-independence holds as predicted in \\S\\ref{subsec:controller}.\nSince pure collapse ($r{=}0$) produces SLV decline from Gen~3 while fixed mix ($r{=}0.5$) yields sustained growth from Gen~2, we bound $0 < r_{\\text{crit}}(\\{2,\\ldots,7\\}) \\leq 0.5$.\n\nDespite sustained spectral expansion, downstream metrics degrade steadily: perplexity increases 33\\% ($9.55 \\to 12.72$) and distinct-4 falls 61\\% ($0.741 \\to 0.290$) over seven generations.\nThis reinforces the asymmetry from \\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}: spectral health under mixed training does not guarantee model health, and the signal attenuation paradox renders per-generation reactive controllers ineffective precisely when collapse prevention succeeds.\n",
```

```json
{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "a5b046f0-daf1-42a7-bc3f-cb9c9b27d9a2",
  "isSidechain": false,

  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex",
    "oldString": "With 50\\% real-data mixing, SLV rises over six of seven generations, reaching 2526 at Gen~7 (57 points above the pure-collapse peak); a negligible dip at Gen~1 ($\\Delta_1 \\approx 0.0003$, well below $\\tau$) reflects the initial fine-tuning transient.",
    "newString": "With 50\\% real-data mixing, SLV rises over nine of ten generations, reaching 2572 at Gen~10 (102 points above the pure-collapse peak); a negligible dip at Gen~1 ($\\Delta_1 \\approx 0.0003$, well below $\\tau$) reflects the initial fine-tuning transient.",
    "originalFile": "\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{subsec:setup}\n\nWe use Pythia-410M-deduped \\cite{biderman2023pythia} as our base model and fine-tune on a 50K-subset of OpenWebText.\nStarting from the pretrained checkpoint (generation~0), each subsequent generation fine-tunes on synthetic text produced by the previous generation, for a total of 10 recursive generations.\nAt each generation, we compute spectral metrics on the embedding Gram matrix $\\mathbf{G} = \\mathbf{E}^\\top \\mathbf{E}$ from 1K held-out samples, where $\\mathbf{E} \\in \\mathbb{R}^{n \\times d}$ stacks last-layer embeddings.\nWe track three spectral indicators: \\textit{effective rank} $r_{\\text{eff}} = \\exp(H(\\mathbf{p}))$, where $H(\\mathbf{p})$ is the Shannon entropy of the normalized singular value distribution; \\textit{spectral log-volume} (SLV) $\\sum_i \\log \\sigma_i = \\frac{1}{2}\\log \\det \\mathbf{G}$; and the leading singular value $\\sigma_1$.\nWe pair these with three downstream metrics: perplexity, HellaSwag accuracy (0-shot), and distinct-4.\n\nFor the mixing comparison (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase1}), we evaluate four strategies sharing the same total real-data budget:\n(1)~\\textbf{pure collapse} ($r_t{=}0$),\n(2)~\\textbf{fixed mix} ($r_t{=}0.50$),\n(3)~\\textbf{linear increasing} ($r_t$: $0.20 \\to 0.80$), and\n(4)~\\textbf{adaptive} ($k{=}10$, Eq.~\\ref{eq:controller}).\n\n\\subsection{Spectral Collapse Characterization}\n\\label{subsec:phase0}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:phase0} reports spectral and downstream metrics across 10 recursive generations.\nThe trajectory reveals a three-phase structure in the spectral dynamics of model collapse.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}rrrrrrc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGen & $r_{\\text{eff}}$ & SLV & PPL & HeSw & D-4 & $\\sigma_1$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n0  & 417.55 & 2366.68 &  9.55 & .413 & .741 & 1942.8 \\\\\n2  & \\textbf{496.44} & \\textbf{2469.93} & 13.58 & .376 & .515 &  712.0 \\\\\n5  & 479.21 & 2370.17 & 24.59 & .344 & .463 &  741.0 \\\\\n7  & 464.20 & 2304.19 & 38.89 & .319 & .447 &  791.3 \\\\\n10 & 442.46 & 2178.95 & 76.78 & .295 & .426 &  825.7 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Spectral and downstream metrics at selected generations (Pythia-410M; full trajectory described in text). Bold marks the spectral peak before collapse onset.}\n\\label{tab:phase0}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Phase I: Spectral Decompression (Gen 0--2).}\nWe identify phase boundaries at inflection points where $r_{\\text{eff}}$ and SLV transition from rising to declining.\nIn the first two generations, $r_{\\text{eff}}$ rises from 417.55 to 496.44 (+18.9\\%) and SLV increases from 2366.68 to 2469.93 (+4.4\\%), while $\\sigma_1$ drops sharply from 1942.8 to 712.0, reflecting a redistribution of spectral mass from the dominant direction to smaller components.\nCrucially, this decompression does not indicate improved model quality: HellaSwag drops from 0.413 to 0.376 ($-$8.9\\%) and distinct-4 falls from 0.741 to 0.515 ($-$30.5\\%) during the same interval.\nHellaSwag already declines 5.6\\% from Gen~0 to Gen~1, precisely when spectral indicators are still rising, implying that decompression captures distributional broadening rather than preservation of task-supporting representations.\nA monitoring system relying solely on spectral metrics during Phase~I would incorrectly conclude the model remains healthy; joint spectral-downstream tracking~\\cite{gu2026sigma} is essential to detect early collapse.\nThe value of SLV lies not in temporal precedence over downstream metrics, but in requiring no text generation or external benchmark evaluation, enabling continuous monitoring at training time.\n\n\\paragraph{Phase II: Collapse Onset (Gen 2--4).}\nBoth $r_{\\text{eff}}$ and SLV reverse after Gen~2 ($r_{\\text{eff}}$: 496.44$\\to$493.31, SLV: 2470$\\to$2441), marking the transition from decompression to collapse while perplexity continues its monotonic rise.\n\n\\paragraph{Phase III: Accelerating Collapse (Gen 4--10).}\nSpectral contraction accelerates: the mean per-generation percentage $r_{\\text{eff}}$ decline in Phase~III is 2.43$\\times$ the onset-generation rate (Gen~2$\\to$3).\nBy Gen~10, $r_{\\text{eff}}$ falls to 442.46 ($-$10.9\\% from peak), SLV drops to 2178.95 ($-$7.9\\% from baseline), perplexity reaches 76.78 ($+$704\\%), and $\\sigma_1$ reverses its earlier decline (700.7$\\to$825.7), indicating renewed spectral concentration (Figure~\\ref{fig:spectral_trajectory}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Scale Validation}\n\\label{subsec:cross_scale}\n\nRepeating the protocol on Pythia-1B (Gen~0--3) confirms the same decompression-onset structure ($r_{\\text{eff}}$ peak at Gen~2, SLV peak one generation earlier), with SLV's low cost and monotonic post-peak decline making it well-suited as an automated collapse monitor.\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Mixing Comparison}\n\\label{subsec:phase1}\n\nUnder pure synthetic training (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}), SLV peaks at Gen~2 (2470) and declines monotonically thereafter.\nWith 50\\% real-data mixing, SLV rises over six of seven generations, reaching 2526 at Gen~7 (57 points above the pure-collapse peak); a negligible dip at Gen~1 ($\\Delta_1 \\approx 0.0003$, well below $\\tau$) reflects the initial fine-tuning transient.\n\nThe adaptive controller ($k{=}10$) remains silent through all seven generations: $\\Delta_t$ is non-positive from Gen~2 onward, and the sole positive value ($\\Delta_1 \\approx 0.0003$) falls $6\\times$ below $\\tau{=}0.002$, leaving the $\\max(0, \\Delta_t - \\tau)$ gate closed.\nFrom Gen~2 onward, $\\tau$-independence holds as predicted in \\S\\ref{subsec:controller}.\nSince $\\Delta_t \\leq 0$ for $t \\geq 2$, the controller remains silent for any finite gain $k$; larger values do not escape the paradox.\nSince pure collapse ($r{=}0$) produces SLV decline from Gen~3 while fixed mix ($r{=}0.5$) yields sustained growth from Gen~2, we bound $0 < r_{\\text{crit}}(\\{2,\\ldots,7\\}) \\leq 0.5$.\n\nDespite sustained spectral expansion, downstream metrics degrade steadily: perplexity increases 33\\% ($9.55 \\to 12.72$) and distinct-4 falls 61\\% ($0.741 \\to 0.290$) over seven generations.\nThis reinforces the asymmetry from \\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}: spectral health under mixed training does not guarantee model health, and the signal attenuation paradox renders per-generation reactive controllers ineffective precisely when collapse prevention succeeds.\n",
```

```json
{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "42f3095c-2c67-43ae-88a5-b9f6f53a5eb7",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "9a666c43-e958-41fd-b079-435eb1ab2b5e",

    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex",
    "oldString": "The adaptive controller ($k{=}10$) remains silent through all seven generations:",
    "newString": "The adaptive controller ($k{=}10$) remains silent through all ten generations:",
    "originalFile": "\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{subsec:setup}\n\nWe use Pythia-410M-deduped \\cite{biderman2023pythia} as our base model and fine-tune on a 50K-subset of OpenWebText.\nStarting from the pretrained checkpoint (generation~0), each subsequent generation fine-tunes on synthetic text produced by the previous generation, for a total of 10 recursive generations.\nAt each generation, we compute spectral metrics on the embedding Gram matrix $\\mathbf{G} = \\mathbf{E}^\\top \\mathbf{E}$ from 1K held-out samples, where $\\mathbf{E} \\in \\mathbb{R}^{n \\times d}$ stacks last-layer embeddings.\nWe track three spectral indicators: \\textit{effective rank} $r_{\\text{eff}} = \\exp(H(\\mathbf{p}))$, where $H(\\mathbf{p})$ is the Shannon entropy of the normalized singular value distribution; \\textit{spectral log-volume} (SLV) $\\sum_i \\log \\sigma_i = \\frac{1}{2}\\log \\det \\mathbf{G}$; and the leading singular value $\\sigma_1$.\nWe pair these with three downstream metrics: perplexity, HellaSwag accuracy (0-shot), and distinct-4.\n\nFor the mixing comparison (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase1}), we evaluate four strategies sharing the same total real-data budget:\n(1)~\\textbf{pure collapse} ($r_t{=}0$),\n(2)~\\textbf{fixed mix} ($r_t{=}0.50$),\n(3)~\\textbf{linear increasing} ($r_t$: $0.20 \\to 0.80$), and\n(4)~\\textbf{adaptive} ($k{=}10$, Eq.~\\ref{eq:controller}).\n\n\\subsection{Spectral Collapse Characterization}\n\\label{subsec:phase0}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:phase0} reports spectral and downstream metrics across 10 recursive generations.\nThe trajectory reveals a three-phase structure in the spectral dynamics of model collapse.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}rrrrrrc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGen & $r_{\\text{eff}}$ & SLV & PPL & HeSw & D-4 & $\\sigma_1$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n0  & 417.55 & 2366.68 &  9.55 & .413 & .741 & 1942.8 \\\\\n2  & \\textbf{496.44} & \\textbf{2469.93} & 13.58 & .376 & .515 &  712.0 \\\\\n5  & 479.21 & 2370.17 & 24.59 & .344 & .463 &  741.0 \\\\\n7  & 464.20 & 2304.19 & 38.89 & .319 & .447 &  791.3 \\\\\n10 & 442.46 & 2178.95 & 76.78 & .295 & .426 &  825.7 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Spectral and downstream metrics at selected generations (Pythia-410M; full trajectory described in text). Bold marks the spectral peak before collapse onset.}\n\\label{tab:phase0}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Phase I: Spectral Decompression (Gen 0--2).}\nWe identify phase boundaries at inflection points where $r_{\\text{eff}}$ and SLV transition from rising to declining.\nIn the first two generations, $r_{\\text{eff}}$ rises from 417.55 to 496.44 (+18.9\\%) and SLV increases from 2366.68 to 2469.93 (+4.4\\%), while $\\sigma_1$ drops sharply from 1942.8 to 712.0, reflecting a redistribution of spectral mass from the dominant direction to smaller components.\nCrucially, this decompression does not indicate improved model quality: HellaSwag drops from 0.413 to 0.376 ($-$8.9\\%) and distinct-4 falls from 0.741 to 0.515 ($-$30.5\\%) during the same interval.\nHellaSwag already declines 5.6\\% from Gen~0 to Gen~1, precisely when spectral indicators are still rising, implying that decompression captures distributional broadening rather than preservation of task-supporting representations.\nA monitoring system relying solely on spectral metrics during Phase~I would incorrectly conclude the model remains healthy; joint spectral-downstream tracking~\\cite{gu2026sigma} is essential to detect early collapse.\nThe value of SLV lies not in temporal precedence over downstream metrics, but in requiring no text generation or external benchmark evaluation, enabling continuous monitoring at training time.\n\n\\paragraph{Phase II: Collapse Onset (Gen 2--4).}\nBoth $r_{\\text{eff}}$ and SLV reverse after Gen~2 ($r_{\\text{eff}}$: 496.44$\\to$493.31, SLV: 2470$\\to$2441), marking the transition from decompression to collapse while perplexity continues its monotonic rise.\n\n\\paragraph{Phase III: Accelerating Collapse (Gen 4--10).}\nSpectral contraction accelerates: the mean per-generation percentage $r_{\\text{eff}}$ decline in Phase~III is 2.43$\\times$ the onset-generation rate (Gen~2$\\to$3).\nBy Gen~10, $r_{\\text{eff}}$ falls to 442.46 ($-$10.9\\% from peak), SLV drops to 2178.95 ($-$7.9\\% from baseline), perplexity reaches 76.78 ($+$704\\%), and $\\sigma_1$ reverses its earlier decline (700.7$\\to$825.7), indicating renewed spectral concentration (Figure~\\ref{fig:spectral_trajectory}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Scale Validation}\n\\label{subsec:cross_scale}\n\nRepeating the protocol on Pythia-1B (Gen~0--3) confirms the same decompression-onset structure ($r_{\\text{eff}}$ peak at Gen~2, SLV peak one generation earlier), with SLV's low cost and monotonic post-peak decline making it well-suited as an automated collapse monitor.\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Mixing Comparison}\n\\label{subsec:phase1}\n\nUnder pure synthetic training (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}), SLV peaks at Gen~2 (2470) and declines monotonically thereafter.\nWith 50\\% real-data mixing, SLV rises over nine of ten generations, reaching 2572 at Gen~10 (102 points above the pure-collapse peak); a negligible dip at Gen~1 ($\\Delta_1 \\approx 0.0003$, well below $\\tau$) reflects the initial fine-tuning transient.\n\nThe adaptive controller ($k{=}10$) remains silent through all seven generations: $\\Delta_t$ is non-positive from Gen~2 onward, and the sole positive value ($\\Delta_1 \\approx 0.0003$) falls $6\\times$ below $\\tau{=}0.002$, leaving the $\\max(0, \\Delta_t - \\tau)$ gate closed.\nFrom Gen~2 onward, $\\tau$-independence holds as predicted in \\S\\ref{subsec:controller}.\nSince $\\Delta_t \\leq 0$ for $t \\geq 2$, the controller remains silent for any finite gain $k$; larger values do not escape the paradox.\nSince pure collapse ($r{=}0$) produces SLV decline from Gen~3 while fixed mix ($r{=}0.5$) yields sustained growth from Gen~2, we bound $0 < r_{\\text{crit}}(\\{2,\\ldots,7\\}) \\leq 0.5$.\n\nDespite sustained spectral expansion, downstream metrics degrade steadily: perplexity increases 33\\% ($9.55 \\to 12.72$) and distinct-4 falls 61\\% ($0.741 \\to 0.290$) over seven generations.\nThis reinforces the asymmetry from \\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}: spectral health under mixed training does not guarantee model health, and the signal attenuation paradox renders per-generation reactive controllers ineffective precisely when collapse prevention succeeds.\n",

      {
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{subsec:setup}\n\nWe use Pythia-410M-deduped \\cite{biderman2023pythia} as our base model and fine-tune on a 50K-subset of OpenWebText.\nStarting from the pretrained checkpoint (generation~0), each subsequent generation fine-tunes on synthetic text produced by the previous generation, for a total of 10 recursive generations.\nAt each generation, we compute spectral metrics on the embedding Gram matrix $\\mathbf{G} = \\mathbf{E}^\\top \\mathbf{E}$ from 1K held-out samples, where $\\mathbf{E} \\in \\mathbb{R}^{n \\times d}$ stacks last-layer embeddings.\nWe track three spectral indicators: \\textit{effective rank} $r_{\\text{eff}} = \\exp(H(\\mathbf{p}))$, where $H(\\mathbf{p})$ is the Shannon entropy of the normalized singular value distribution; \\textit{spectral log-volume} (SLV) $\\sum_i \\log \\sigma_i = \\frac{1}{2}\\log \\det \\mathbf{G}$; and the leading singular value $\\sigma_1$.\nWe pair these with three downstream metrics: perplexity, HellaSwag accuracy (0-shot), and distinct-4.\n\nFor the mixing comparison (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase1}), we evaluate four strategies sharing the same total real-data budget:\n(1)~\\textbf{pure collapse} ($r_t{=}0$),\n(2)~\\textbf{fixed mix} ($r_t{=}0.50$),\n(3)~\\textbf{linear increasing} ($r_t$: $0.20 \\to 0.80$), and\n(4)~\\textbf{adaptive} ($k{=}10$, Eq.~\\ref{eq:controller}).\n\n\\subsection{Spectral Collapse Characterization}\n\\label{subsec:phase0}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:phase0} reports spectral and downstream metrics across 10 recursive generations.\nThe trajectory reveals a three-phase structure in the spectral dynamics of model collapse.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}rrrrrrc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGen & $r_{\\text{eff}}$ & SLV & PPL & HeSw & D-4 & $\\sigma_1$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n0  & 417.55 & 2366.68 &  9.55 & .413 & .741 & 1942.8 \\\\\n2  & \\textbf{496.44} & \\textbf{2469.93} & 13.58 & .376 & .515 &  712.0 \\\\\n5  & 479.21 & 2370.17 & 24.59 & .344 & .463 &  741.0 \\\\\n7  & 464.20 & 2304.19 & 38.89 & .319 & .447 &  791.3 \\\\\n10 & 442.46 & 2178.95 & 76.78 & .295 & .426 &  825.7 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Spectral and downstream metrics at selected generations (Pythia-410M; full trajectory described in text). Bold marks the spectral peak before collapse onset.}\n\\label{tab:phase0}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Phase I: Spectral Decompression (Gen 0--2).}\nWe identify phase boundaries at inflection points where $r_{\\text{eff}}$ and SLV transition from rising to declining.\nIn the first two generations, $r_{\\text{eff}}$ rises from 417.55 to 496.44 (+18.9\\%) and SLV increases from 2366.68 to 2469.93 (+4.4\\%), while $\\sigma_1$ drops sharply from 1942.8 to 712.0, reflecting a redistribution of spectral mass from the dominant direction to smaller components.\nCrucially, this decompression does not indicate improved model quality: HellaSwag drops from 0.413 to 0.376 ($-$8.9\\%) and distinct-4 falls from 0.741 to 0.515 ($-$30.5\\%) during the same interval.\nHellaSwag already declines 5.6\\% from Gen~0 to Gen~1, precisely when spectral indicators are still rising, implying that decompression captures distributional broadening rather than preservation of task-supporting representations.\nA monitoring system relying solely on spectral metrics during Phase~I would incorrectly conclude the model remains healthy; joint spectral-downstream tracking~\\cite{gu2026sigma} is essential to detect early collapse.\nThe value of SLV lies not in temporal precedence over downstream metrics, but in requiring no text generation or external benchmark evaluation, enabling continuous monitoring at training time.\n\n\\paragraph{Phase II: Collapse Onset (Gen 2--4).}\nBoth $r_{\\text{eff}}$ and SLV reverse after Gen~2 ($r_{\\text{eff}}$: 496.44$\\to$493.31, SLV: 2470$\\to$2441), marking the transition from decompression to collapse while perplexity continues its monotonic rise.\n\n\\paragraph{Phase III: Accelerating Collapse (Gen 4--10).}\nSpectral contraction accelerates: the mean per-generation percentage $r_{\\text{eff}}$ decline in Phase~III is 2.43$\\times$ the onset-generation rate (Gen~2$\\to$3).\nBy Gen~10, $r_{\\text{eff}}$ falls to 442.46 ($-$10.9\\% from peak), SLV drops to 2178.95 ($-$7.9\\% from baseline), perplexity reaches 76.78 ($+$704\\%), and $\\sigma_1$ reverses its earlier decline (700.7$\\to$825.7), indicating renewed spectral concentration (Figure~\\ref{fig:spectral_trajectory}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Scale Validation}\n\\label{subsec:cross_scale}\n\nRepeating the protocol on Pythia-1B (Gen~0--3) confirms the same decompression-onset structure ($r_{\\text{eff}}$ peak at Gen~2, SLV peak one generation earlier), with SLV's low cost and monotonic post-peak decline making it well-suited as an automated collapse monitor.\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Mixing Comparison}\n\\label{subsec:phase1}\n\nUnder pure synthetic training (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}), SLV peaks at Gen~2 (2470) and declines monotonically thereafter.\nWith 50\\% real-data mixing, SLV rises over nine of ten generations, reaching 2572 at Gen~10 (102 points above the pure-collapse peak); a negligible dip at Gen~1 ($\\Delta_1 \\approx 0.0003$, well below $\\tau$) reflects the initial fine-tuning transient.\n\nThe adaptive controller ($k{=}10$) remains silent through all ten generations: $\\Delta_t$ is non-positive from Gen~2 onward, and the sole positive value ($\\Delta_1 \\approx 0.0003$) falls $6\\times$ below $\\tau{=}0.002$, leaving the $\\max(0, \\Delta_t - \\tau)$ gate closed.\nFrom Gen~2 onward, $\\tau$-independence holds as predicted in \\S\\ref{subsec:controller}.\nSince $\\Delta_t \\leq 0$ for $t \\geq 2$, the controller remains silent for any finite gain $k$; larger values do not escape the paradox.\nSince pure collapse ($r{=}0$) produces SLV decline from Gen~3 while fixed mix ($r{=}0.5$) yields sustained growth from Gen~2, we bound $0 < r_{\\text{crit}}(\\{2,\\ldots,10\\}) \\leq 0.5$.\n\nDespite sustained spectral expansion, downstream metrics degrade steadily: perplexity increases 33\\% ($9.55 \\to 12.72$) and distinct-4 falls 61\\% ($0.741 \\to 0.290$) over seven generations.\nThis reinforces the asymmetry from \\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}: spectral health under mixed training does not guarantee model health, and the signal attenuation paradox renders per-generation reactive controllers ineffective precisely when collapse prevention succeeds.\n",
```

```json
    "oldString": "over Gen~2--7 for Pythia-410M on OpenWebText.",
    "newString": "over Gen~2--10 for Pythia-410M on OpenWebText.",
    "originalFile": "\\subsection{Spectral Metrics}\n\\label{subsec:spectral_metrics}\n\nGiven a language model at generation $t$, we extract last-layer embeddings for $n{=}1{,}000$ held-out samples to form $\\mathbf{E} \\in \\mathbb{R}^{n \\times d}$ and compute the Gram matrix $\\mathbf{G} = \\mathbf{E}^\\top \\mathbf{E}$.\nLet $\\sigma_1 \\geq \\sigma_2 \\geq \\cdots \\geq \\sigma_d$ denote the singular values of $\\mathbf{E}$.\nWe track three complementary spectral indicators:\n\n\\paragraph{Spectral Log-Volume (SLV).}\n$\\text{SLV} = \\sum_i \\log \\sigma_i = \\frac{1}{2} \\log \\det \\mathbf{G}$.\nThis quantity measures the total volume spanned by the embedding cloud in log-space.\nBecause it sums over all singular values, SLV is sensitive to contraction along any spectral direction, not only the dominant ones.\n\n\\paragraph{Effective Rank.}\n$r_{\\text{eff}} = \\exp(H(\\mathbf{p}))$, where $p_i = \\sigma_i / \\sum_j \\sigma_j$ and $H$ is the Shannon entropy, measuring how many dimensions carry substantial variance (uniform $\\to d$, concentrated $\\to 1$).\n\n\\paragraph{Leading Singular Value.}\n$\\sigma_1$ tracks the dominant spectral direction; rising $\\sigma_1$ under falling $r_{\\text{eff}}$ signals representational collapse into a low-dimensional subspace.\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Mixing Controller}\n\\label{subsec:controller}\n\nWe propose a closed-loop controller that adjusts the real-data mixing ratio $r_t$ at each generation based on the rate of spectral change.\nThe update rule is:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:controller}\nr_{t+1} = \\frac{B_{\\text{rem}}}{T - t} + k \\cdot \\max(0,\\; \\Delta_t - \\tau)\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $B_{\\text{rem}}$ is the remaining real-data budget (fraction of total budget not yet consumed), $T$ is the total number of generations, $\\tau{=}0.002$ is a sensitivity threshold, and $k{=}10$ is the controller gain.\nThe signal $\\Delta_t$ is the baseline-normalized SLV drop: $\\Delta_t = (\\widetilde{S}_{t-1} - \\widetilde{S}_t) / |\\widetilde{S}_0|$, where $\\widetilde{S}_t$ is the EMA-smoothed SLV ($\\alpha{=}0.5$) and $\\widetilde{S}_0$ is initialized from the pretrained model (Gen~0, SLV${=}2366.68$).\nThe signed form ensures that spectral decompression ($\\Delta_t < 0$) does not trigger the controller; only contraction activates the $\\max(0, \\cdot)$ gate in Eq.~\\ref{eq:controller}.\nThe output is clipped to $[r_{\\min}, r_{\\max}] = [0.20, 0.80]$ and subject to the global constraint $\\sum_t r_t \\leq B_{\\text{total}}$.\n\nThe first term distributes the remaining budget uniformly across future generations, ensuring graceful degradation even without spectral signal.\nThe second term injects additional real data when SLV contraction exceeds the threshold $\\tau$, proportional to the severity of the spectral drop.\nThis design requires no lookahead and operates with a single hyperparameter choice ($k$) beyond the budget constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Controller Silence Condition.}\nDefine $r_{\\text{crit}}(T)$ as the minimum constant mixing ratio such that SLV is non-decreasing from the second generation onward: $\\widetilde{S}_t \\geq \\widetilde{S}_{t-1}$ for all $t \\in \\{2, \\ldots, T\\}$ (excluding the initial fine-tuning transient at $t{=}1$).\nWhen a constant ratio $r \\geq r_{\\text{crit}}$ is applied, $\\Delta_t \\leq 0 < \\tau$ for all $t \\geq 2$ and any $\\tau > 0$, so the controller boost is identically zero.\nThis $\\tau$-independence means the paradox cannot be resolved by lowering the sensitivity threshold; the same argument extends to cumulative variants.\nOur experiments (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase1}) empirically bound $0 < r_{\\text{crit}} \\leq 0.5$ over Gen~2--7 for Pythia-410M on OpenWebText.\n",
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01W9U9BH6cbbzmEJS6KXnAcA`</summary>

```text
1	
2	We presented a systematic spectral characterization of model collapse across 10 recursive generations of Pythia-410M, revealing a three-phase trajectory (decompression, onset, 2.43$\times$ acceleration) confirmed at the 1B scale.
3	SLV is particularly suited for automated monitoring: it requires only a forward pass, declines monotonically after its peak, and captures volume changes before they manifest as dimensionality reduction.
4	A proof-of-concept proportional controller exposes a signal attenuation paradox: moderate real-data mixing suppresses spectral collapse onset entirely, rendering reactive controllers silent~\cite{scheffer2009ews}.
5	Future work should explore cumulative indicators, validate at larger scales and longer horizons, and develop controller architectures robust to their own intervention effects.
6	
7	\paragraph{Limitations.}
8	All experiments use a single random seed; multi-seed replication is needed to establish statistical robustness.
9	We validate at two model scales only (Pythia-410M and a 1B pilot through Gen~3), leaving open whether the three-phase pattern holds at larger scales or with different architectures.
10	Ten recursive generations may be shorter than production pipelines, and the spectral trajectory beyond this horizon is unknown.
11	
```

_source line 1324 · 2026-05-18T09:31:30.204Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01QVocR3yYn7xQT6Eh5gVkqY`</summary>

```text
1	Recursive synthetic training, where each model generation fine-tunes on its predecessor's outputs, causes model collapse: a progressive loss of distributional diversity and downstream capability.
2	Detecting collapse currently requires costly text generation or benchmark evaluation at every generation.
3	Spectral analysis of internal representations offers a cheaper alternative, but the spectral dynamics of recursive collapse lack systematic characterization, and no prior work connects spectral indicators to closed-loop prevention.
4	We track spectral properties of the embedding Gram matrix across 10 recursive generations of Pythia-410M, with pilot validation on Pythia-1B.
5	Our analysis reveals a reproducible three-phase pattern: spectral decompression (generations 0--2), where effective rank and spectral log-volume (SLV) increase even as downstream performance degrades; collapse onset (generations 2--4), where spectral indicators reverse; and accelerating collapse (generations 4--10), where spectral contraction proceeds at 2.43$\times$ the onset-generation rate.
6	Among the indicators we examine, SLV is particularly suited for automated monitoring: it requires only a forward pass on held-out data, captures distributed volume changes across all spectral directions, and exhibits post-decompression monotonic decline amenable to threshold-based control.
7	We further design a proof-of-concept proportional controller and discover a signal attenuation paradox: moderate real-data mixing prevents the spectral contraction that would activate the controller, rendering reactive spectral feedback ineffective precisely when prevention succeeds.
8	
```

### Tool result

_source line 1326 · 2026-05-18T09:31:30.714Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_016PdcyXyyXtsJALRukUYsEZ`</summary>

```text
1	
2	Synthetic data has become integral to language model training.
3	Instruction tuning, knowledge distillation, and data augmentation all routinely use model-generated text, and as synthetic corpora are recycled into training pipelines, the process often becomes recursive: each generation of models is fine-tuned on outputs from the previous one.
4	Recent work demonstrates that such recursive synthetic training triggers \textit{model collapse}, a progressive erosion of distributional tails and downstream capability~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024tails, alemohammad2024mad}.
5	The problem is especially pressing as web-scraped corpora increasingly contain model-generated content, a trend that introduces implicit recursive loops even without deliberate data reuse.
6	
7	While model collapse is now well-characterized through theoretical analysis~\cite{dohmatob2024tails} and empirical studies~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024mad}, its effect on the geometric structure of learned representations remains poorly understood.
8	Characterizing how the embedding space contracts during recursive training could yield computationally cheap collapse indicators from internal model states alone, without text generation or benchmark evaluation.
9	
10	Current collapse detection depends on downstream metrics such as perplexity and benchmark accuracy, or on analysis of generated text through lexical diversity measures.
11	Both approaches require either costly inference or external evaluation infrastructure at every generation.
12	SIGMA~\cite{gu2026sigma} introduced spectral monitoring through embedding covariance analysis and showed that spectral indicators can track collapse, but validated only on OPT-125M over short generation horizons.
13	Three gaps remain open.
14	First, no work has systematically characterized spectral dynamics beyond a few generations at 410M+ parameters.
15	Second, the temporal relationship between spectral indicators and downstream degradation has not been examined.
16	Third, no study has connected spectral indicators to closed-loop collapse prevention through the lens of early warning signals~\cite{scheffer2009ews}.
17	
18	We address these gaps by tracking spectral indicators of the embedding Gram matrix across 10 recursive generations of Pythia-410M-deduped~\cite{biderman2023pythia}, with pilot validation at the 1B scale.
19	Our analysis reveals a reproducible three-phase pattern (Figure~\ref{fig:spectral_trajectory}): spectral decompression (Gen~0--2), where effective rank ($r_{\text{eff}}$) and spectral log-volume (SLV) rise while downstream performance already declines; collapse onset (Gen~2--4), where spectral indicators reverse; and accelerating collapse (Gen~4--10), where contraction proceeds at 2.43$\times$ the onset-generation rate.
20	Among these indicators, SLV is particularly suited for automated monitoring: it requires only a forward pass on held-out data (no text generation), captures distributed volume changes across all spectral directions, and declines monotonically after its peak.
21	We further design a proof-of-concept proportional controller and discover a signal attenuation paradox: moderate real-data mixing prevents the spectral contraction that would trigger the controller, rendering reactive spectral feedback ineffective precisely when collapse prevention succeeds.
22	
23	Our contributions are:
24	\begin{itemize}
25	\item \textbf{C1:} The first joint spectral-downstream characterization at 410M+ scale, revealing a three-phase collapse pattern (decompression, onset, 2.43$\times$ acceleration) across 10 generations (\S\ref{subsec:phase0}, Table~\ref{tab:phase0}).
26	\item \textbf{C2:} SLV as a computationally cheap collapse monitor with monotonic post-peak decline and a HellaSwag-spectral asymmetry showing downstream degradation precedes spectral collapse (\S\ref{subsec:phase0}).
27	\item \textbf{C3:} A signal attenuation paradox: moderate real-data mixing suppresses the spectral signal reactive controllers depend on, a structural limitation formalized via $r_{\text{crit}}$ (\S\ref{subsec:controller}, \S\ref{subsec:phase1}).
28	\end{itemize}
29	
```

```json
      "content": "\nSynthetic data has become integral to language model training.\nInstruction tuning, knowledge distillation, and data augmentation all routinely use model-generated text, and as synthetic corpora are recycled into training pipelines, the process often becomes recursive: each generation of models is fine-tuned on outputs from the previous one.\nRecent work demonstrates that such recursive synthetic training triggers \\textit{model collapse}, a progressive erosion of distributional tails and downstream capability~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024tails, alemohammad2024mad}.\nThe problem is especially pressing as web-scraped corpora increasingly contain model-generated content, a trend that introduces implicit recursive loops even without deliberate data reuse.\n\nWhile model collapse is now well-characterized through theoretical analysis~\\cite{dohmatob2024tails} and empirical studies~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024mad}, its effect on the geometric structure of learned representations remains poorly understood.\nCharacterizing how the embedding space contracts during recursive training could yield computationally cheap collapse indicators from internal model states alone, without text generation or benchmark evaluation.\n\nCurrent collapse detection depends on downstream metrics such as perplexity and benchmark accuracy, or on analysis of generated text through lexical diversity measures.\nBoth approaches require either costly inference or external evaluation infrastructure at every generation.\nSIGMA~\\cite{gu2026sigma} introduced spectral monitoring through embedding covariance analysis and showed that spectral indicators can track collapse, but validated only on OPT-125M over short generation horizons.\nThree gaps remain open.\nFirst, no work has systematically characterized spectral dynamics beyond a few generations at 410M+ parameters.\nSecond, the temporal relationship between spectral indicators and downstream degradation has not been examined.\nThird, no study has connected spectral indicators to closed-loop collapse prevention through the lens of early warning signals~\\cite{scheffer2009ews}.\n\nWe address these gaps by tracking spectral indicators of the embedding Gram matrix across 10 recursive generations of Pythia-410M-deduped~\\cite{biderman2023pythia}, with pilot validation at the 1B scale.\nOur analysis reveals a reproducible three-phase pattern (Figure~\\ref{fig:spectral_trajectory}): spectral decompression (Gen~0--2), where effective rank ($r_{\\text{eff}}$) and spectral log-volume (SLV) rise while downstream performance already declines; collapse onset (Gen~2--4), where spectral indicators reverse; and accelerating collapse (Gen~4--10), where contraction proceeds at 2.43$\\times$ the onset-generation rate.\nAmong these indicators, SLV is particularly suited for automated monitoring: it requires only a forward pass on held-out data (no text generation), captures distributed volume changes across all spectral directions, and declines monotonically after its peak.\nWe further design a proof-of-concept proportional controller and discover a signal attenuation paradox: moderate real-data mixing prevents the spectral contraction that would trigger the controller, rendering reactive spectral feedback ineffective precisely when collapse prevention succeeds.\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{itemize}\n\\item \\textbf{C1:} The first joint spectral-downstream characterization at 410M+ scale, revealing a three-phase collapse pattern (decompression, onset, 2.43$\\times$ acceleration) across 10 generations (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}, Table~\\ref{tab:phase0}).\n\\item \\textbf{C2:} SLV as a computationally cheap collapse monitor with monotonic post-peak decline and a HellaSwag-spectral asymmetry showing downstream degradation precedes spectral collapse (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}).\n\\item \\textbf{C3:} A signal attenuation paradox: moderate real-data mixing suppresses the spectral signal reactive controllers depend on, a structural limitation formalized via $r_{\\text{crit}}$ (\\S\\ref{subsec:controller}, \\S\\ref{subsec:phase1}).\n\\end{itemize}\n",
      "numLines": 29,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 29
    }
  },
  "sourceToolAssistantUUID": "25c55bd9-6957-4480-a53b-cf1c55e2fd5b",
```

```text
1	\subsection{Spectral Metrics}
2	\label{subsec:spectral_metrics}
3	
4	Given a language model at generation $t$, we extract last-layer embeddings for $n{=}1{,}000$ held-out samples to form $\mathbf{E} \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times d}$ and compute the Gram matrix $\mathbf{G} = \mathbf{E}^\top \mathbf{E}$.
5	Let $\sigma_1 \geq \sigma_2 \geq \cdots \geq \sigma_d$ denote the singular values of $\mathbf{E}$.
6	We track three complementary spectral indicators:
7	
8	\paragraph{Spectral Log-Volume (SLV).}
9	$\text{SLV} = \sum_i \log \sigma_i = \frac{1}{2} \log \det \mathbf{G}$.
10	This quantity measures the total volume spanned by the embedding cloud in log-space.
11	Because it sums over all singular values, SLV is sensitive to contraction along any spectral direction, not only the dominant ones.
12	
13	\paragraph{Effective Rank.}
14	$r_{\text{eff}} = \exp(H(\mathbf{p}))$, where $p_i = \sigma_i / \sum_j \sigma_j$ and $H$ is the Shannon entropy, measuring how many dimensions carry substantial variance (uniform $\to d$, concentrated $\to 1$).
15	
16	\paragraph{Leading Singular Value.}
17	$\sigma_1$ tracks the dominant spectral direction; rising $\sigma_1$ under falling $r_{\text{eff}}$ signals representational collapse into a low-dimensional subspace.
18	
19	\subsection{Adaptive Mixing Controller}
20	\label{subsec:controller}
21	
22	We propose a closed-loop controller that adjusts the real-data mixing ratio $r_t$ at each generation based on the rate of spectral change.
23	The update rule is:
24	\begin{equation}
25	\label{eq:controller}
26	r_{t+1} = \frac{B_{\text{rem}}}{T - t} + k \cdot \max(0,\; \Delta_t - \tau)
27	\end{equation}
28	where $B_{\text{rem}}$ is the remaining real-data budget (fraction of total budget not yet consumed), $T$ is the total number of generations, $\tau{=}0.002$ is a sensitivity threshold, and $k{=}10$ is the controller gain.
29	The signal $\Delta_t$ is the baseline-normalized SLV drop: $\Delta_t = (\widetilde{S}_{t-1} - \widetilde{S}_t) / |\widetilde{S}_0|$, where $\widetilde{S}_t$ is the EMA-smoothed SLV ($\alpha{=}0.5$) and $\widetilde{S}_0$ is initialized from the pretrained model (Gen~0, SLV${=}2366.68$).
30	The signed form ensures that spectral decompression ($\Delta_t < 0$) does not trigger the controller; only contraction activates the $\max(0, \cdot)$ gate in Eq.~\ref{eq:controller}.
31	The output is clipped to $[r_{\min}, r_{\max}] = [0.20, 0.80]$ and subject to the global constraint $\sum_t r_t \leq B_{\text{total}}$.
32	
33	The first term distributes the remaining budget uniformly across future generations, ensuring graceful degradation even without spectral signal.
34	The second term injects additional real data when SLV contraction exceeds the threshold $\tau$, proportional to the severity of the spectral drop.
35	This design requires no lookahead and operates with a single hyperparameter choice ($k$) beyond the budget constraint.
36	
37	\paragraph{Controller Silence Condition.}
38	Define $r_{\text{crit}}(T)$ as the minimum constant mixing ratio such that SLV is non-decreasing from the second generation onward: $\widetilde{S}_t \geq \widetilde{S}_{t-1}$ for all $t \in \{2, \ldots, T\}$ (excluding the initial fine-tuning transient at $t{=}1$).
39	When a constant ratio $r \geq r_{\text{crit}}$ is applied, $\Delta_t \leq 0 < \tau$ for all $t \geq 2$ and any $\tau > 0$, so the controller boost is identically zero.
40	This $\tau$-independence means the paradox cannot be resolved by lowering the sensitivity threshold; the same argument extends to cumulative variants.
41	Our experiments (\S\ref{subsec:phase1}) empirically bound $0 < r_{\text{crit}} \leq 0.5$ over Gen~2--10 for Pythia-410M on OpenWebText.
42	
```

```text
1	
2	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
3	\label{subsec:setup}
4	
5	We use Pythia-410M-deduped \cite{biderman2023pythia} as our base model and fine-tune on a 50K-subset of OpenWebText.
6	Starting from the pretrained checkpoint (generation~0), each subsequent generation fine-tunes on synthetic text produced by the previous generation, for a total of 10 recursive generations.
7	At each generation, we compute spectral metrics on the embedding Gram matrix $\mathbf{G} = \mathbf{E}^\top \mathbf{E}$ from 1K held-out samples, where $\mathbf{E} \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times d}$ stacks last-layer embeddings.
8	We track three spectral indicators: \textit{effective rank} $r_{\text{eff}} = \exp(H(\mathbf{p}))$, where $H(\mathbf{p})$ is the Shannon entropy of the normalized singular value distribution; \textit{spectral log-volume} (SLV) $\sum_i \log \sigma_i = \frac{1}{2}\log \det \mathbf{G}$; and the leading singular value $\sigma_1$.
9	We pair these with three downstream metrics: perplexity, HellaSwag accuracy (0-shot), and distinct-4.
10	
11	For the mixing comparison (\S\ref{subsec:phase1}), we evaluate four strategies sharing the same total real-data budget:
12	(1)~\textbf{pure collapse} ($r_t{=}0$),
13	(2)~\textbf{fixed mix} ($r_t{=}0.50$),
14	(3)~\textbf{linear increasing} ($r_t$: $0.20 \to 0.80$), and
15	(4)~\textbf{adaptive} ($k{=}10$, Eq.~\ref{eq:controller}).
16	
17	\subsection{Spectral Collapse Characterization}
18	\label{subsec:phase0}
19	
20	Table~\ref{tab:phase0} reports spectral and downstream metrics across 10 recursive generations.
21	The trajectory reveals a three-phase structure in the spectral dynamics of model collapse.
22	
23	\begin{table}[t]
24	\centering
25	\small
26	\begin{tabular}{@{}rrrrrrc@{}}
27	\toprule
28	Gen & $r_{\text{eff}}$ & SLV & PPL & HeSw & D-4 & $\sigma_1$ \\
29	\midrule
30	0  & 417.55 & 2366.68 &  9.55 & .413 & .741 & 1942.8 \\
31	2  & \textbf{496.44} & \textbf{2469.93} & 13.58 & .376 & .515 &  712.0 \\
32	5  & 479.21 & 2370.17 & 24.59 & .344 & .463 &  741.0 \\
33	7  & 464.20 & 2304.19 & 38.89 & .319 & .447 &  791.3 \\
34	10 & 442.46 & 2178.95 & 76.78 & .295 & .426 &  825.7 \\
35	\bottomrule
36	\end{tabular}
37	\caption{Spectral and downstream metrics at selected generations (Pythia-410M; full trajectory described in text). Bold marks the spectral peak before collapse onset.}
38	\label{tab:phase0}
39	\end{table}
40	
41	\paragraph{Phase I: Spectral Decompression (Gen 0--2).}
42	We identify phase boundaries at inflection points where $r_{\text{eff}}$ and SLV transition from rising to declining.
43	In the first two generations, $r_{\text{eff}}$ rises from 417.55 to 496.44 (+18.9\%) and SLV increases from 2366.68 to 2469.93 (+4.4\%), while $\sigma_1$ drops sharply from 1942.8 to 712.0, reflecting a redistribution of spectral mass from the dominant direction to smaller components.
44	Crucially, this decompression does not indicate improved model quality: HellaSwag drops from 0.413 to 0.376 ($-$8.9\%) and distinct-4 falls from 0.741 to 0.515 ($-$30.5\%) during the same interval.
45	HellaSwag already declines 5.6\% from Gen~0 to Gen~1, precisely when spectral indicators are still rising, implying that decompression captures distributional broadening rather than preservation of task-supporting representations.
46	A monitoring system relying solely on spectral metrics during Phase~I would incorrectly conclude the model remains healthy; joint spectral-downstream tracking~\cite{gu2026sigma} is essential to detect early collapse.
47	The value of SLV lies not in temporal precedence over downstream metrics, but in requiring no text generation or external benchmark evaluation, enabling continuous monitoring at training time.
48	
49	\paragraph{Phase II: Collapse Onset (Gen 2--4).}
50	Both $r_{\text{eff}}$ and SLV reverse after Gen~2 ($r_{\text{eff}}$: 496.44$\to$493.31, SLV: 2470$\to$2441), marking the transition from decompression to collapse while perplexity continues its monotonic rise.
51	
52	\paragraph{Phase III: Accelerating Collapse (Gen 4--10).}
53	Spectral contraction accelerates: the mean per-generation percentage $r_{\text{eff}}$ decline in Phase~III is 2.43$\times$ the onset-generation rate (Gen~2$\to$3).
54	By Gen~10, $r_{\text{eff}}$ falls to 442.46 ($-$10.9\% from peak), SLV drops to 2178.95 ($-$7.9\% from baseline), perplexity reaches 76.78 ($+$704\%), and $\sigma_1$ reverses its earlier decline (700.7$\to$825.7), indicating renewed spectral concentration (Figure~\ref{fig:spectral_trajectory}).
55	
56	\subsection{Cross-Scale Validation}
57	\label{subsec:cross_scale}
58	
59	Repeating the protocol on Pythia-1B (Gen~0--3) confirms the same decompression-onset structure ($r_{\text{eff}}$ peak at Gen~2, SLV peak one generation earlier), with SLV's low cost and monotonic post-peak decline making it well-suited as an automated collapse monitor.
60	
61	\subsection{Adaptive Mixing Comparison}
62	\label{subsec:phase1}
63	
64	Under pure synthetic training (\S\ref{subsec:phase0}), SLV peaks at Gen~2 (2470) and declines monotonically thereafter.
65	With 50\% real-data mixing, SLV rises over nine of ten generations, reaching 2572 at Gen~10 (102 points above the pure-collapse peak); a negligible dip at Gen~1 ($\Delta_1 \approx 0.0003$, well below $\tau$) reflects the initial fine-tuning transient.
66	
67	The adaptive controller ($k{=}10$) remains silent through all ten generations: $\Delta_t$ is non-positive from Gen~2 onward, and the sole positive value ($\Delta_1 \approx 0.0003$) falls $6\times$ below $\tau{=}0.002$, leaving the $\max(0, \Delta_t - \tau)$ gate closed.
68	From Gen~2 onward, $\tau$-independence holds as predicted in \S\ref{subsec:controller}.
69	Since $\Delta_t \leq 0$ for $t \geq 2$, the controller remains silent for any finite gain $k$; larger values do not escape the paradox.
70	Since pure collapse ($r{=}0$) produces SLV decline from Gen~3 while fixed mix ($r{=}0.5$) yields sustained growth from Gen~2, we bound $0 < r_{\text{crit}}(\{2,\ldots,10\}) \leq 0.5$.
71	
72	Despite sustained spectral expansion, downstream metrics degrade steadily: perplexity increases 58\% ($9.55 \to 15.10$) and distinct-4 falls 68\% ($0.741 \to 0.239$) over ten generations.
73	This reinforces the asymmetry from \S\ref{subsec:phase0}: spectral health under mixed training does not guarantee model health, and the signal attenuation paradox renders per-generation reactive controllers ineffective precisely when collapse prevention succeeds.
74	
```

```json
      "content": "\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{subsec:setup}\n\nWe use Pythia-410M-deduped \\cite{biderman2023pythia} as our base model and fine-tune on a 50K-subset of OpenWebText.\nStarting from the pretrained checkpoint (generation~0), each subsequent generation fine-tunes on synthetic text produced by the previous generation, for a total of 10 recursive generations.\nAt each generation, we compute spectral metrics on the embedding Gram matrix $\\mathbf{G} = \\mathbf{E}^\\top \\mathbf{E}$ from 1K held-out samples, where $\\mathbf{E} \\in \\mathbb{R}^{n \\times d}$ stacks last-layer embeddings.\nWe track three spectral indicators: \\textit{effective rank} $r_{\\text{eff}} = \\exp(H(\\mathbf{p}))$, where $H(\\mathbf{p})$ is the Shannon entropy of the normalized singular value distribution; \\textit{spectral log-volume} (SLV) $\\sum_i \\log \\sigma_i = \\frac{1}{2}\\log \\det \\mathbf{G}$; and the leading singular value $\\sigma_1$.\nWe pair these with three downstream metrics: perplexity, HellaSwag accuracy (0-shot), and distinct-4.\n\nFor the mixing comparison (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase1}), we evaluate four strategies sharing the same total real-data budget:\n(1)~\\textbf{pure collapse} ($r_t{=}0$),\n(2)~\\textbf{fixed mix} ($r_t{=}0.50$),\n(3)~\\textbf{linear increasing} ($r_t$: $0.20 \\to 0.80$), and\n(4)~\\textbf{adaptive} ($k{=}10$, Eq.~\\ref{eq:controller}).\n\n\\subsection{Spectral Collapse Characterization}\n\\label{subsec:phase0}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:phase0} reports spectral and downstream metrics across 10 recursive generations.\nThe trajectory reveals a three-phase structure in the spectral dynamics of model collapse.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}rrrrrrc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGen & $r_{\\text{eff}}$ & SLV & PPL & HeSw & D-4 & $\\sigma_1$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n0  & 417.55 & 2366.68 &  9.55 & .413 & .741 & 1942.8 \\\\\n2  & \\textbf{496.44} & \\textbf{2469.93} & 13.58 & .376 & .515 &  712.0 \\\\\n5  & 479.21 & 2370.17 & 24.59 & .344 & .463 &  741.0 \\\\\n7  & 464.20 & 2304.19 & 38.89 & .319 & .447 &  791.3 \\\\\n10 & 442.46 & 2178.95 & 76.78 & .295 & .426 &  825.7 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Spectral and downstream metrics at selected generations (Pythia-410M; full trajectory described in text). Bold marks the spectral peak before collapse onset.}\n\\label{tab:phase0}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Phase I: Spectral Decompression (Gen 0--2).}\nWe identify phase boundaries at inflection points where $r_{\\text{eff}}$ and SLV transition from rising to declining.\nIn the first two generations, $r_{\\text{eff}}$ rises from 417.55 to 496.44 (+18.9\\%) and SLV increases from 2366.68 to 2469.93 (+4.4\\%), while $\\sigma_1$ drops sharply from 1942.8 to 712.0, reflecting a redistribution of spectral mass from the dominant direction to smaller components.\nCrucially, this decompression does not indicate improved model quality: HellaSwag drops from 0.413 to 0.376 ($-$8.9\\%) and distinct-4 falls from 0.741 to 0.515 ($-$30.5\\%) during the same interval.\nHellaSwag already declines 5.6\\% from Gen~0 to Gen~1, precisely when spectral indicators are still rising, implying that decompression captures distributional broadening rather than preservation of task-supporting representations.\nA monitoring system relying solely on spectral metrics during Phase~I would incorrectly conclude the model remains healthy; joint spectral-downstream tracking~\\cite{gu2026sigma} is essential to detect early collapse.\nThe value of SLV lies not in temporal precedence over downstream metrics, but in requiring no text generation or external benchmark evaluation, enabling continuous monitoring at training time.\n\n\\paragraph{Phase II: Collapse Onset (Gen 2--4).}\nBoth $r_{\\text{eff}}$ and SLV reverse after Gen~2 ($r_{\\text{eff}}$: 496.44$\\to$493.31, SLV: 2470$\\to$2441), marking the transition from decompression to collapse while perplexity continues its monotonic rise.\n\n\\paragraph{Phase III: Accelerating Collapse (Gen 4--10).}\nSpectral contraction accelerates: the mean per-generation percentage $r_{\\text{eff}}$ decline in Phase~III is 2.43$\\times$ the onset-generation rate (Gen~2$\\to$3).\nBy Gen~10, $r_{\\text{eff}}$ falls to 442.46 ($-$10.9\\% from peak), SLV drops to 2178.95 ($-$7.9\\% from baseline), perplexity reaches 76.78 ($+$704\\%), and $\\sigma_1$ reverses its earlier decline (700.7$\\to$825.7), indicating renewed spectral concentration (Figure~\\ref{fig:spectral_trajectory}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Scale Validation}\n\\label{subsec:cross_scale}\n\nRepeating the protocol on Pythia-1B (Gen~0--3) confirms the same decompression-onset structure ($r_{\\text{eff}}$ peak at Gen~2, SLV peak one generation earlier), with SLV's low cost and monotonic post-peak decline making it well-suited as an automated collapse monitor.\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Mixing Comparison}\n\\label{subsec:phase1}\n\nUnder pure synthetic training (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}), SLV peaks at Gen~2 (2470) and declines monotonically thereafter.\nWith 50\\% real-data mixing, SLV rises over nine of ten generations, reaching 2572 at Gen~10 (102 points above the pure-collapse peak); a negligible dip at Gen~1 ($\\Delta_1 \\approx 0.0003$, well below $\\tau$) reflects the initial fine-tuning transient.\n\nThe adaptive controller ($k{=}10$) remains silent through all ten generations: $\\Delta_t$ is non-positive from Gen~2 onward, and the sole positive value ($\\Delta_1 \\approx 0.0003$) falls $6\\times$ below $\\tau{=}0.002$, leaving the $\\max(0, \\Delta_t - \\tau)$ gate closed.\nFrom Gen~2 onward, $\\tau$-independence holds as predicted in \\S\\ref{subsec:controller}.\nSince $\\Delta_t \\leq 0$ for $t \\geq 2$, the controller remains silent for any finite gain $k$; larger values do not escape the paradox.\nSince pure collapse ($r{=}0$) produces SLV decline from Gen~3 while fixed mix ($r{=}0.5$) yields sustained growth from Gen~2, we bound $0 < r_{\\text{crit}}(\\{2,\\ldots,10\\}) \\leq 0.5$.\n\nDespite sustained spectral expansion, downstream metrics degrade steadily: perplexity increases 58\\% ($9.55 \\to 15.10$) and distinct-4 falls 68\\% ($0.741 \\to 0.239$) over ten generations.\nThis reinforces the asymmetry from \\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}: spectral health under mixed training does not guarantee model health, and the signal attenuation paradox renders per-generation reactive controllers ineffective precisely when collapse prevention succeeds.\n",
      "numLines": 74,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 74
    }
  },
```

```json
    "originalFile": "Recursive synthetic training, where each model generation fine-tunes on its predecessor's outputs, causes model collapse: a progressive loss of distributional diversity and downstream capability.\nDetecting collapse currently requires costly text generation or benchmark evaluation at every generation.\nSpectral analysis of internal representations offers a cheaper alternative, but the spectral dynamics of recursive collapse lack systematic characterization, and no prior work connects spectral indicators to closed-loop prevention.\nWe track spectral properties of the embedding Gram matrix across 10 recursive generations of Pythia-410M, with pilot validation on Pythia-1B.\nOur analysis reveals a reproducible three-phase pattern: spectral decompression (generations 0--2), where effective rank and spectral log-volume (SLV) increase even as downstream performance degrades; collapse onset (generations 2--4), where spectral indicators reverse; and accelerating collapse (generations 4--10), where spectral contraction proceeds at 2.43$\\times$ the onset-generation rate.\nAmong the indicators we examine, SLV is particularly suited for automated monitoring: it requires only a forward pass on held-out data, captures distributed volume changes across all spectral directions, and exhibits post-decompression monotonic decline amenable to threshold-based control.\nWe further design a proof-of-concept proportional controller and discover a signal attenuation paradox: moderate real-data mixing prevents the spectral contraction that would activate the controller, rendering reactive spectral feedback ineffective precisely when prevention succeeds.\n",
```

```json
    "newString": "We further design a proof-of-concept proportional controller and discover a signal attenuation paradox: moderate real-data mixing prevents the spectral contraction that would trigger the controller, so reactive spectral feedback becomes ineffective precisely when collapse prevention succeeds.",
    "originalFile": "\nSynthetic data has become integral to language model training.\nInstruction tuning, knowledge distillation, and data augmentation all routinely use model-generated text, and as synthetic corpora are recycled into training pipelines, the process often becomes recursive: each generation of models is fine-tuned on outputs from the previous one.\nRecent work demonstrates that such recursive synthetic training triggers \\textit{model collapse}, a progressive erosion of distributional tails and downstream capability~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024tails, alemohammad2024mad}.\nThe problem is especially pressing as web-scraped corpora increasingly contain model-generated content, a trend that introduces implicit recursive loops even without deliberate data reuse.\n\nWhile model collapse is now well-characterized through theoretical analysis~\\cite{dohmatob2024tails} and empirical studies~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024mad}, its effect on the geometric structure of learned representations remains poorly understood.\nCharacterizing how the embedding space contracts during recursive training could yield computationally cheap collapse indicators from internal model states alone, without text generation or benchmark evaluation.\n\nCurrent collapse detection depends on downstream metrics such as perplexity and benchmark accuracy, or on analysis of generated text through lexical diversity measures.\nBoth approaches require either costly inference or external evaluation infrastructure at every generation.\nSIGMA~\\cite{gu2026sigma} introduced spectral monitoring through embedding covariance analysis and showed that spectral indicators can track collapse, but validated only on OPT-125M over short generation horizons.\nThree gaps remain open.\nFirst, no work has systematically characterized spectral dynamics beyond a few generations at 410M+ parameters.\nSecond, the temporal relationship between spectral indicators and downstream degradation has not been examined.\nThird, no study has connected spectral indicators to closed-loop collapse prevention through the lens of early warning signals~\\cite{scheffer2009ews}.\n\nWe address these gaps by tracking spectral indicators of the embedding Gram matrix across 10 recursive generations of Pythia-410M-deduped~\\cite{biderman2023pythia}, with pilot validation at the 1B scale.\nOur analysis reveals a reproducible three-phase pattern (Figure~\\ref{fig:spectral_trajectory}): spectral decompression (Gen~0--2), where effective rank ($r_{\\text{eff}}$) and spectral log-volume (SLV) rise while downstream performance already declines; collapse onset (Gen~2--4), where spectral indicators reverse; and accelerating collapse (Gen~4--10), where contraction proceeds at 2.43$\\times$ the onset-generation rate.\nAmong these indicators, SLV is particularly suited for automated monitoring: it requires only a forward pass on held-out data (no text generation), captures distributed volume changes across all spectral directions, and declines monotonically after its peak.\nWe further design a proof-of-concept proportional controller and discover a signal attenuation paradox: moderate real-data mixing prevents the spectral contraction that would trigger the controller, rendering reactive spectral feedback ineffective precisely when collapse prevention succeeds.\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{itemize}\n\\item \\textbf{C1:} The first joint spectral-downstream characterization at 410M+ scale, revealing a three-phase collapse pattern (decompression, onset, 2.43$\\times$ acceleration) across 10 generations (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}, Table~\\ref{tab:phase0}).\n\\item \\textbf{C2:} SLV as a computationally cheap collapse monitor with monotonic post-peak decline and a HellaSwag-spectral asymmetry showing downstream degradation precedes spectral collapse (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}).\n\\item \\textbf{C3:} A signal attenuation paradox: moderate real-data mixing suppresses the spectral signal reactive controllers depend on, a structural limitation formalized via $r_{\\text{crit}}$ (\\S\\ref{subsec:controller}, \\S\\ref{subsec:phase1}).\n\\end{itemize}\n",
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\subsection{Spectral Metrics}\n\\label{subsec:spectral_metrics}\n\nGiven a language model at generation $t$, we extract last-layer embeddings for $n{=}1{,}000$ held-out samples to form $\\mathbf{E} \\in \\mathbb{R}^{n \\times d}$ and compute the Gram matrix $\\mathbf{G} = \\mathbf{E}^\\top \\mathbf{E}$.\nLet $\\sigma_1 \\geq \\sigma_2 \\geq \\cdots \\geq \\sigma_d$ denote the singular values of $\\mathbf{E}$.\nWe track three complementary spectral indicators:\n\n\\paragraph{Spectral Log-Volume (SLV).}\n$\\text{SLV} = \\sum_i \\log \\sigma_i = \\frac{1}{2} \\log \\det \\mathbf{G}$.\nThis quantity measures the total volume spanned by the embedding cloud in log-space.\nBecause it sums over all singular values, SLV is sensitive to contraction along any spectral direction, not only the dominant ones.\n\n\\paragraph{Effective Rank.}\n$r_{\\text{eff}} = \\exp(H(\\mathbf{p}))$, where $p_i = \\sigma_i / \\sum_j \\sigma_j$ and $H$ is the Shannon entropy, measuring how many dimensions carry substantial variance (uniform $\\to d$, concentrated $\\to 1$).\n\n\\paragraph{Leading Singular Value.}\n$\\sigma_1$ tracks the dominant spectral direction; rising $\\sigma_1$ under falling $r_{\\text{eff}}$ signals representational collapse into a low-dimensional subspace.\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Mixing Controller}\n\\label{subsec:controller}\n\nWe propose a closed-loop controller that adjusts the real-data mixing ratio $r_t$ at each generation based on the rate of spectral change.\nThe update rule is:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:controller}\nr_{t+1} = \\frac{B_{\\text{rem}}}{T - t} + k \\cdot \\max(0,\\; \\Delta_t - \\tau)\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $B_{\\text{rem}}$ is the remaining real-data budget (fraction of total budget not yet consumed), $T$ is the total number of generations, $\\tau{=}0.002$ is a sensitivity threshold, and $k{=}10$ is the controller gain.\nThe signal $\\Delta_t$ is the baseline-normalized SLV drop: $\\Delta_t = (\\widetilde{S}_{t-1} - \\widetilde{S}_t) / |\\widetilde{S}_0|$, where $\\widetilde{S}_t$ is the EMA-smoothed SLV ($\\alpha{=}0.5$) and $\\widetilde{S}_0$ is initialized from the pretrained model (Gen~0, SLV${=}2366.68$).\nThe signed form ensures that spectral decompression ($\\Delta_t < 0$) does not trigger the controller; only contraction activates the $\\max(0, \\cdot)$ gate in Eq.~\\ref{eq:controller}.\nThe output is clipped to $[r_{\\min}, r_{\\max}] = [0.20, 0.80]$ and subject to the global constraint $\\sum_t r_t \\leq B_{\\text{total}}$.\n\nThe first term distributes the remaining budget uniformly across future generations, ensuring graceful degradation even without spectral signal.\nThe second term injects additional real data when SLV contraction exceeds the threshold $\\tau$, proportional to the severity of the spectral drop.\nThis design requires no lookahead and operates with a single hyperparameter choice ($k$) beyond the budget constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Controller Silence Condition.}\nDefine $r_{\\text{crit}}(T)$ as the minimum constant mixing ratio such that SLV is non-decreasing from the second generation onward: $\\widetilde{S}_t \\geq \\widetilde{S}_{t-1}$ for all $t \\in \\{2, \\ldots, T\\}$ (excluding the initial fine-tuning transient at $t{=}1$).\nWhen a constant ratio $r \\geq r_{\\text{crit}}$ is applied, $\\Delta_t \\leq 0 < \\tau$ for all $t \\geq 2$ and any $\\tau > 0$, so the controller boost is identically zero.\nThis $\\tau$-independence means the paradox cannot be resolved by lowering the sensitivity threshold; the same argument extends to cumulative variants.\nOur experiments (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase1}) empirically bound $0 < r_{\\text{crit}} \\leq 0.5$ over Gen~2--10 for Pythia-410M on OpenWebText.\n",
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{subsec:setup}\n\nWe use Pythia-410M-deduped \\cite{biderman2023pythia} as our base model and fine-tune on a 50K-subset of OpenWebText.\nStarting from the pretrained checkpoint (generation~0), each subsequent generation fine-tunes on synthetic text produced by the previous generation, for a total of 10 recursive generations.\nAt each generation, we compute spectral metrics on the embedding Gram matrix $\\mathbf{G} = \\mathbf{E}^\\top \\mathbf{E}$ from 1K held-out samples, where $\\mathbf{E} \\in \\mathbb{R}^{n \\times d}$ stacks last-layer embeddings.\nWe track three spectral indicators: \\textit{effective rank} $r_{\\text{eff}} = \\exp(H(\\mathbf{p}))$, where $H(\\mathbf{p})$ is the Shannon entropy of the normalized singular value distribution; \\textit{spectral log-volume} (SLV) $\\sum_i \\log \\sigma_i = \\frac{1}{2}\\log \\det \\mathbf{G}$; and the leading singular value $\\sigma_1$.\nWe pair these with three downstream metrics: perplexity, HellaSwag accuracy (0-shot), and distinct-4.\n\nFor the mixing comparison (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase1}), we evaluate four strategies sharing the same total real-data budget:\n(1)~\\textbf{pure collapse} ($r_t{=}0$),\n(2)~\\textbf{fixed mix} ($r_t{=}0.50$),\n(3)~\\textbf{linear increasing} ($r_t$: $0.20 \\to 0.80$), and\n(4)~\\textbf{adaptive} ($k{=}10$, Eq.~\\ref{eq:controller}).\n\n\\subsection{Spectral Collapse Characterization}\n\\label{subsec:phase0}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:phase0} reports spectral and downstream metrics across 10 recursive generations.\nThe trajectory reveals a three-phase structure in the spectral dynamics of model collapse.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}rrrrrrc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGen & $r_{\\text{eff}}$ & SLV & PPL & HeSw & D-4 & $\\sigma_1$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n0  & 417.55 & 2366.68 &  9.55 & .413 & .741 & 1942.8 \\\\\n2  & \\textbf{496.44} & \\textbf{2469.93} & 13.58 & .376 & .515 &  712.0 \\\\\n5  & 479.21 & 2370.17 & 24.59 & .344 & .463 &  741.0 \\\\\n7  & 464.20 & 2304.19 & 38.89 & .319 & .447 &  791.3 \\\\\n10 & 442.46 & 2178.95 & 76.78 & .295 & .426 &  825.7 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Spectral and downstream metrics at selected generations (Pythia-410M; full trajectory described in text). Bold marks the spectral peak before collapse onset.}\n\\label{tab:phase0}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Phase I: Spectral Decompression (Gen 0--2).}\nWe identify phase boundaries at inflection points where $r_{\\text{eff}}$ and SLV transition from rising to declining.\nIn the first two generations, $r_{\\text{eff}}$ rises from 417.55 to 496.44 (+18.9\\%) and SLV increases from 2366.68 to 2469.93 (+4.4\\%), while $\\sigma_1$ drops sharply from 1942.8 to 712.0, reflecting a redistribution of spectral mass from the dominant direction to smaller components.\nCrucially, this decompression does not indicate improved model quality: HellaSwag drops from 0.413 to 0.376 ($-$8.9\\%) and distinct-4 falls from 0.741 to 0.515 ($-$30.5\\%) during the same interval.\nHellaSwag already declines 5.6\\% from Gen~0 to Gen~1, precisely when spectral indicators are still rising, implying that decompression captures distributional broadening rather than preservation of task-supporting representations.\nA monitoring system relying solely on spectral metrics during Phase~I would incorrectly conclude the model remains healthy; joint spectral-downstream tracking~\\cite{gu2026sigma} is essential to detect early collapse.\nThe value of SLV lies not in temporal precedence over downstream metrics, but in requiring no text generation or external benchmark evaluation, enabling continuous monitoring at training time.\n\n\\paragraph{Phase II: Collapse Onset (Gen 2--4).}\nBoth $r_{\\text{eff}}$ and SLV reverse after Gen~2 ($r_{\\text{eff}}$: 496.44$\\to$493.31, SLV: 2470$\\to$2441), marking the transition from decompression to collapse while perplexity continues its monotonic rise.\n\n\\paragraph{Phase III: Accelerating Collapse (Gen 4--10).}\nSpectral contraction accelerates: the mean per-generation percentage $r_{\\text{eff}}$ decline in Phase~III is 2.43$\\times$ the onset-generation rate (Gen~2$\\to$3).\nBy Gen~10, $r_{\\text{eff}}$ falls to 442.46 ($-$10.9\\% from peak), SLV drops to 2178.95 ($-$7.9\\% from baseline), perplexity reaches 76.78 ($+$704\\%), and $\\sigma_1$ reverses its earlier decline (700.7$\\to$825.7), indicating renewed spectral concentration (Figure~\\ref{fig:spectral_trajectory}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Scale Validation}\n\\label{subsec:cross_scale}\n\nRepeating the protocol on Pythia-1B (Gen~0--3) confirms the same decompression-onset structure ($r_{\\text{eff}}$ peak at Gen~2, SLV peak one generation earlier), with SLV's low cost and monotonic post-peak decline making it well-suited as an automated collapse monitor.\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Mixing Comparison}\n\\label{subsec:phase1}\n\nUnder pure synthetic training (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}), SLV peaks at Gen~2 (2470) and declines monotonically thereafter.\nWith 50\\% real-data mixing, SLV rises over nine of ten generations, reaching 2572 at Gen~10 (102 points above the pure-collapse peak); a negligible dip at Gen~1 ($\\Delta_1 \\approx 0.0003$, well below $\\tau$) reflects the initial fine-tuning transient.\n\nThe adaptive controller ($k{=}10$) remains silent through all ten generations: $\\Delta_t$ is non-positive from Gen~2 onward, and the sole positive value ($\\Delta_1 \\approx 0.0003$) falls $6\\times$ below $\\tau{=}0.002$, leaving the $\\max(0, \\Delta_t - \\tau)$ gate closed.\nFrom Gen~2 onward, $\\tau$-independence holds as predicted in \\S\\ref{subsec:controller}.\nSince $\\Delta_t \\leq 0$ for $t \\geq 2$, the controller remains silent for any finite gain $k$; larger values do not escape the paradox.\nSince pure collapse ($r{=}0$) produces SLV decline from Gen~3 while fixed mix ($r{=}0.5$) yields sustained growth from Gen~2, we bound $0 < r_{\\text{crit}}(\\{2,\\ldots,10\\}) \\leq 0.5$.\n\nDespite sustained spectral expansion, downstream metrics degrade steadily: perplexity increases 58\\% ($9.55 \\to 15.10$) and distinct-4 falls 68\\% ($0.741 \\to 0.239$) over ten generations.\nThis reinforces the asymmetry from \\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}: spectral health under mixed training does not guarantee model health, and the signal attenuation paradox renders per-generation reactive controllers ineffective precisely when collapse prevention succeeds.\n",
```
